❌ Status: INCORRECT

🤖 archi's Answer
On FASRC Cannon, you submit GPU jobs to the **`gpu_test`** partition. You request GPUs using the **`--gres=gpu:<N>`** flag, where `<N>` is the number of GPUs you need (e.g., `--gres=gpu:1` for one GPU) [VSCode Remote Development via SSH and Tunnel](https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/vscode-remote-development-via-ssh-or-tunnel/). ### Example `salloc` command: ```bash salloc --partition gpu_test --gres=gpu:1 --time 01:00:00 --mem 4GB ``` ### Example `sbatch` script: ```bash #!/bin/bash #SBATCH --partition gpu_test #SBATCH --gres=gpu:1 #SBATCH --time 01:00:00 #SBATCH --mem 4GB # Your GPU job commands here ``` For more details on available partitions and GPU resources, see the [GPU Computing on the FASRC cluster](https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/gpgpu-computing-on-the-cluster/) documentation.
✅ Expected Answer
Use the gpu partition (or gpu_test for short test jobs). Request GPUs with --gres=gpu:N in your SLURM submit script, e.g. --gres=gpu:1.

Question 107: question_107

❓ Question

Where on the FASRC Cannon filesystem should I store large datasets that I don't need backed up?

🎯 Retrieval Check

Expected Document(s): https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/cluster-storage

Retrieved Documents: docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/quickstart-guide, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/fasrc-cluster-storage-policy, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/fasse, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/iqss-cannon-quickstart-guide, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/policy-scratch, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/cluster-storage, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/r-parallel, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/hsph-research-computing-overview

✅ Status: FULLY CORRECT

🤖 archi's Answer
For large datasets you don't need backed up, store them on the **scratch filesystem** at `/n/netscratch`. Key points about scratch: - It's a large, shared temporary filesystem designed for high I/O jobs - It is **not backed up** — files older than 90 days will be deleted through a periodic purge process, which can run at any time (especially if scratch is getting full) - Each lab is allotted 50TB of scratch space - There is no charge for netscratch For more details, see the [Scratch](https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/policy-scratch) documentation. If you need more context on FASRC's storage hierarchy (home, lab, scratch, etc.), check out the [Data Storage Workflow](https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/data-storage-workflow-rdm/) page.
✅ Expected Answer
Use /n/holyscratch01/<lab>/ for fast scratch storage. It is not backed up and is periodically purged of older files.
🎯 Expected Source Documents
  • https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/cluster-storage

🤖 Assistant Message #3

Key points about scratch: - It's a large, shared temporary filesystem designed for high I/O jobs - It is **not backed up** — files older than 90 days will be deleted through a periodic purge process, which can run at any time (especially if scratch is getting full) - Each lab is allotted 50TB of scratch space - There is no charge for netscratch For more details, see the [Scratch](https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/policy-scratch) documentation. If you need more context on FASRC's storage hierarchy (home, lab, scratch, etc.), check out the [Data Storage Workflow](https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/data-storage-workflow-rdm/) page.

Question 108: question_108

❓ Question

My SLURM job on Cannon was killed with an out-of-memory error. How do I diagnose what the actual memory peak was, and how do I request more memory for the next submission?

🎯 Retrieval Check

Expected Document(s): https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs

Retrieved Documents: docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/faq, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/slurm-memory-limits, github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/Training/Advanced_Cluster_Usage/Exercise2/C/README.md, github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/Training/Advanced_Cluster_Usage/Exercise2/R/README.md, github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/Training/Advanced_Cluster_Usage/Exercise2/Python/README.md

❌ Status: INCORRECT

🤖 archi's Answer
Here’s how to diagnose the memory peak and request more memory for Cannon: ### 1. Diagnose the actual memory peak * **Run `jobstats` on the failed job:** ```bash jobstats <JOBID> ``` This shows the memory usage for the job. Note that if the job was killed by SLURM for exceeding its limit, the recorded memory peak may be inaccurate. For the most accurate measurement, run the job to completion with a generous memory limit and then check `jobstats` again. * **Check job state with `sacct`:** ```bash sacct -j <JOBID> ``` A job killed for memory will show a state like `OUT_OF_ME+` and an exit code indicating failure. ### 2. Request more memory for the next submission Add one of the following to your `#SBATCH` directives: * **Per-node memory:** ```bash #SBATCH --mem 4G # 4 GB per node ``` You can use `M` (MB) or `G` (GB) suffixes. * **Per-CPU memory (alternative):** ```bash #SBATCH --mem-per-cpu 2G # 2 GB per allocated CPU ``` ### Tips * Start with a generous limit, then use `jobstats` to see what the job actually used, and adjust downward for efficiency. * The default SLURM memory limit is very small (100 MB per node), so most jobs need an explicit `--mem` or `--mem-per-cpu` setting. * For multi-node jobs, `--mem` is the maximum memory used on **any one node**.
✅ Expected Answer
After the job ends, run sacct -j <jobid> --format=JobID,MaxRSS,ReqMem to see the peak memory the job used (MaxRSS) versus what was requested (ReqMem). In your next submit script, increase the memory request via --mem=<N>G (whole-job) or --mem-per-cpu=<N>G (per-task), aiming a bit above the observed MaxRSS so future variations don't kill the job again.
🎯 Expected Source Documents
  • https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs

Question 109: question_109

❓ Question

I want to run a multi-node MPI program on Cannon that uses one GPU per node. What does the SLURM submit script need to specify, and how do I launch the MPI ranks?

🎯 Retrieval Check

Expected Document(s): https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs, https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/mpi-message-passing-interface

Retrieved Documents: github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/GPU_Computing/Dist_GPU_Computing/README.md, github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/GPU_Computing/Dist_GPU_Computing/NCCL_and_CUDA/MultiNode/README.md, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/r-parallel, github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/Training/Distributed_GPU_Computing/Exercise5/README.md, github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/GPU_Computing/Dist_GPU_Computing/NCCL_and_CUDA/MultiNode/ncclAllGather_mpi.cu, github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/.github/workflows/Languages-Python-Example1.yml, github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/Parallel_Computing/Parallel_HDF5/Example2/README.md, github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/Example_Recipes/Recipe3/README.md, github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/Libraries/README.md, github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/Example_Recipes/README.md

❌ Status: INCORRECT

🤖 archi's Answer
The agent attempted to retrieve documentation for running distributed GPU workloads on the Cannon cluster, specifically searching for SLURM submission scripts (`run.sbatch`) and examples of multi-node MPI+CUDA or NCCL+CUDA implementations. The search returned relevant resources from the FAS Research Computing documentation, including examples for PyTorch DDP and NCCL bootstrapping. **Summary of the SLURM Script Requirements and MPI Launch for Cannon:** To run a multi-node MPI program with one GPU per node on Cannon, your SLURM script (`run.sbatch`) must specify the following key elements: 1. **Resource Allocation**: * Use `#SBATCH --nodes=<N>` to specify the number of nodes. * Use `#SBATCH --ntasks=<N>` to set the total number of MPI ranks (typically equal to the number of nodes if using one GPU per node). * Use `#SBATCH --gpus-per-node=1` to request exactly one GPU per node. This ensures each MPI rank is bound to a single GPU. * Specify the partition (e.g., `#SBATCH --partition=gpu`) and time limits as needed. 2. **Environment Setup**: * Load necessary modules for MPI (e.g., OpenMPI or MVAPICH2) and CUDA/NCCL if required by your application. * Ensure the environment variables for GPU visibility (like `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES`) are handled correctly, often automatically by SLURM when `--gpus-per-node` is used, but sometimes requiring explicit export. 3. **Launching the MPI Ranks**: * Use `srun` to launch the application. The command typically looks like: ```bash srun --ntasks=$SLURM_NTASKS --gpus-per-task=1 ./your_mpi_program ``` * If using NCCL for collective operations, you may need to broadcast an `ncclUniqueId` via MPI before initializing NCCL communicators. The retrieved documentation highlights examples using `MPI_Bcast` for this purpose. **Note:** This run stopped after hitting the recursion limit of 50, preventing further tool calls or detailed code generation. The above answer is synthesized from the retrieved documentation snippets regarding distributed GPU computing on Cannon.
✅ Expected Answer
In your submit script: choose a GPU-enabled partition (e.g. gpu), set --nodes=<N> with --ntasks-per-node=1 and --gres=gpu:1 (per-node GPU request), load the MPI and CUDA modules with module load. Launch the program with srun (not mpirun directly) so SLURM places one rank per node with the bound GPU; e.g. srun --mpi=pmix ./my_program. Use sinfo and scontrol show partition gpu to confirm node configuration before submitting.
